The Nature of « Bond; Bill or Obligation, and Diff 
refions for the true making thereof, = R 
| / 
1. A Bond, BIT or Obligation, is a Deed in Writings, 
and the Nature thereof is to bind one Man to another, by" 
two or more, or many, (as occaſion is) to pay a Sum oi 
Money, or to give, da or perform ſomething, whereuj 
on it is defined to be the Right of a Perſon, by which 
hath another Perſon bound unto him to pay that whic 
he oweth him. Right therefore is the chiefeſt Cauſec 
an Obligation; the AQ of a Man that ſeals and deliver 
ſuch Obligation, is only the remote ox ſecondary Cauſe 
New, that which is called an Obligation, is the um. 
with that which is commonly and vulgarly called an 
termed a Bond ; and it is alſo the ſame with a Bill; a 
the Lawyers make this difference between them, (vis Mrecy 
when iris in Engliſb it is called a Bill, and when it is Mu 
L «tin, a Bond or Obi ei Som che Lacin Word =

Direct ions for ſuing out Fines and making of Con- | 
cords thereof; with ſeveral neceſſary Notes there- | 
* | 


is 2 a Fine, Finalis Concordia, quia imponit finem 1 
itibus. | 
' Fines are Inſtruments on Record, of A ts con- 
cerning Lands or other Hereditaments made moſt uſually 
pon Writs of Covenant in the Court of Common Pleas, \ 
nd are of ſuch Force and Efficacy in Law, that they are 
perpetual Bars to all Parties to the ſame Fine, and Eſtran · 
gers, not proſecuring their Right in due Time. | 
He who acknowledgeth the Fine, is called the Conuſor ; 14 
ind he w whom the Pine is levied, is called the Conuſee. 3 
If the uſe of a Fine be not declared before nor after the | 
Fine levied, it ſhall be intended to the uſe of the Cenuſor, 
and his Heirs, A 
Infants (thats, all Perſons under the Age of One and 
lwenty Years) ought to have ſpecial Care how they levy 
ines, for unleſs they be reverſed by the Infants them- 
ſelves before they come of Age, they are good. 


If 


go The Young Clerk's Tutor enlarged. 


If a married Woman under Age levy a Fine of her own 
Lands, ſhe may not reverſe it, unleſs her Husband die 
before ſhe come to full Age. A A 

If a married Woman levy a Fine of her Jointure, ſhe 
will thereby loſe both that and her Thirds, if rhe Join. 
ture were well ſettled before Marriage. Gy 

It is requiſite, that either the Conuſdr or the Conuſce 
be ſeized of the Land in the Fine, at the Time of levying ff ji 
thereof, otherwiſe the Fine is void. | 2 

Perſons outlawed, or waved in perſonal Actions, may. 
alien by Fine.- 

To ſue out a Fine by Dedimus poteftatem to Commiſſion. 
ers in the Country, you are firſt to draw your Præcipe o 
the Writ of Covenant in Paper, and then write under 
your Commiſſioners Names, four or more, whereof one is 
to be a Knight, thus: 


Suſſ: . Præcipe A. B. quod juſte, &c. reddat C. D. Con. &c. 
de uno Meſſuagio, uno horreo, uno gardino, & decem acris ter. 
cum pertin. in E. 


De. jo. F. D. Mil. H. J. L. M. N. O. P. Q Cf 


Carry this to the Curſitor of the ſame County, and he 
will make your Dedimus poteſtatem to take the Fine. . 

When yon have your Dedimus poteflatem, then make 
ready your Concord to be fairly engroſſed in Parchment, 
and fee that the Land be exactly named in the Decd. The 
Forms of which Concords next follow. |

Note, That where there be divers Conuſots, the Releaſe 


and Warranty muſt be from the Heirs of one of the Conu- 


ſors only, as in the laſt mentioned; and alſo when a Fine 
15 levied to divers Conuſees, the Right ſhall be limited to 


one of them only, and the Remiſe and Warranty to his 
Heirs only, whoſe Right it is acknowledged to be.
